REGISTER
VOLUME 13 t l i n d e n NUMBER 169O /S /it E D
Washington, Saturday, August 28, 1948
„ TITLE 3— THE PRESIDENT
EXECUTIVE ORDER 9991
Creating an E mergency B oard T o I nves ­
tigate a D ispute  B etween  T he P itts ­
burgh &  West V irginia R ailway C om­
pany  and C ertain of its E mployees
WHEREAS a dispute exists between  
the Pittsburgh &  West Virginia Railway 
Company, a carrier, and certain of its  
employees represented by the Brother­
hood of Railroad Trainmen, a labor or­
ganization; and
WHEREAS this dispute has not here­
tofore been adjusted under the provisions  
of the Railway Labor Act, as amended;  
and
WHEREAS this dispute, in the judg­
ment of the National Mediation Board,  
threatens substantially to interrupt in­
terstate commerce within the States of  
Ohio and Pennsylvania to a degree such  
as* to deprive those states of essential  
transportation service:
NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of the  
authority vested in me by section 10 of  
the Railway Labor Act, as amended (45  
U. S. C. ICO), I hereby create a board  
of three members,%to be appointed by me, 
to investigate the said dispute. No mem­
ber of the said board shall be pecuniarily  
or otherwise interested in any organiza­
tion of railway employees or any carrier.
The board shall report its findings to  
the President with respect to the said  
dispute within thirty days from the date  
of this order.
As provided by section 10 of the Rail­
way Labor Act, as amended, from this  
date and for thirty days after the board  
has made its report to the President, no  
change, except by agreement, shall be  
made by the Pittsburgh &  West Virginia 
Railway Company or its employees in the  
conditions out of which the said dispute  
arose.
Harry S. T ruman
The W hite H ouse,
August 26, 1948.
[P. R. Doc. 48-7744; Filed, Aug. 26, 1948;
3:32 p. m.]
TITLE 7— AGRICULTURE
Chapter VIII— Production and Market­
ing Administration (Sugar Branch)
[General Sugar Quota Regs., Series 10, No. 1,  
Amdt. 5]
Part 821—S ugar Q uotas
SUGAR QUOTAS FOR 1 9 4 8
Basis and purpose.  This amendment  
is issued pursuant to the Sugar Act of  
1948 and is made merely for the purpose  
of correcting an error in the tabulation  
of additional prorations for certain for­
eign countries appearing in Amendment  
4 to General Sugar Quota Regulations,  
Series, 10, No. 1. In view of the nature  
of this amendment it is determined and  
found that compliance with the notice,  
procedure and effective date require­
ments of the Administrative Procedure  
Act is unnecessary. Also, since the error  
hereby corrected was made in Amend­
ment 4 to General Sugar Quota Regula­
tions, Series 10, No. 1, it shall become ef­
fective on the same date as such Amend­
ment 4 becomes effective.
By virtue of the authority vested in the  
Secretary of Agriculture by the Sugar  
Act of 1948 (61 Stat. 922) and the  
Administrative Procedure Act (60 Stat.  
237) General Sugar Quota Regulations,  
Series 10, No. 1 (13 F. R. 133) as amended  
(13 F. R. 1303, 3109, 4009, 4660) are here­
by further amended to substitute in the  
tabulation set forth in §  821.6 (b) the  
figure “391,119” in lieu of “403,785” as  
the additional proration of sugar in  
pounds, raw value, to Venezuela.
Statement of bases and considerations.  
The statement of bases and considera­
tions published with Amendment 4 to  
General Sugar Quota Regulations, Series  
10, No. 1, stated that the additional defi­
cit of 50,000 short tons of sugar estab­
lished thereby for the Republic of the  
Philippines was being prorated to Cuba  
and other foreign countries on the basis  
of 95 percent to Cuba and' 5 percent to  
such other foreign countries and that  
the additional quota for such foreign  
countries other than Cuba was being pro­
rated, pursuant to section 204 (d) of the  
Sugar Act of 1948, to the Dominican Re­
public, Peru and El Salvador. Since no  
additional proration was being made to  
Venezuela, the tabulationjn §  821.6 (b)

Recommendation 
Since the agreement recommended here would not be subject to 
change until the fall of 1968 and since the Carrier offered an addi- 
tional holiday last December as part of a three year agreement, the 
parties should negotiate a mutually agreeable additional paid holiday 
into their contract.
```

[F. R. Doc. 48-152; Piled, Jfan. 2, 1948;

EXECUTIVE ORDER 9918
C reating  an  E m ergency  B oard to  I n ves ­
tigate  D isputes  B e t w e e n the  A la ­
bama , T ennessee  and  N orthern  
R ailroad  C o m p a n y and  O ther  C ar­
riers , and  C ertain  of  T heir  E m plo yees
WHEREAS disputes exist between the 
Alabama, Tennessee and Northern Rail­
road Company and certain other car­
riers designated in list A attached hereto 
and made a part hereof, and certain of 
their employees represented by the 
seventeen cooperating nonoperating la­
bor organizations’ designated in list B 
attached hereto and made a part hereof; 
and
WHEREAS these disputes have not 
heretofore been adjusted under the pro­
visions of the Railway Labor Act, as 
amended; and
WHEREAS these disputes, in the 
judgment of the National Mediation 
Board, threaten substantially to inter­
rupt interstate commerce to a -degree 
such as to deprive the country of essen­
tial transportation service:
NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of the 
authority vested in me by section 10 of 
the Railway Labor Act, as amended (45 
U. S. C. 160), I  hereby create a board of 
three members, to be appointed by me, 
to investigate the said disputes.
Each of the carriers involved shall be 
given independent consideration in such 
investigation, and the said labor organi­
zations shall be permitted to function
FEDERAL REGISTER
as a unit in proceedings before the 
board.
No member of the said board shall be 
pecuniarily or otherwise interested in 
any organization of railway employees 
or any carrier.
The board shall report its findings to 
the President with respect to the said 
disputes within thirty days from the 
date of this order. The report of the 
board shall contain separate and inde­
pendent findings with respect to each of 
the carriers involved.
As provided by section 10 of the Rail­
way Labor Act, as amended, from this 
date and for thirty days after the board 
has made its report to the President, no 
change, except by agreement, shall be 
made by any of the carriers involved or 
their employees in the conditions out of 
which the said disputes arose.
H arry  S. T r um an
T he  W h it e H ouse ,
December 31,1947.

P art  91—L e n d ing P olicies
REGIONAL AGRICULTURAL CREDIT CORPORATION 
OF WASHINGTON, D. C,
Title 6, Chapter I, of the Code of Fed­
eral Regulations, is hereby revised in the 
following respects:
1. Paragraph (f) of § 2.1 Organization,  
is amended to read as follows:
(f) The board of directors of the Re­
gional Agricultural Credit Corporation of 
Washington, D. C., directs and super­
vises the affairs of that Corporation un­
der 47 Stat. 713, as amended; 12 U. S. C., 
Chap. 7, Subchap. Vin. The Regional 
Agricultural Credit Corporation of Wash­
ington, D. C., has its headquarters with 
the Farm Credit Administration in 
Washington, D. C., and ^utilizes the per­
sonnel and facilities of the Farm Credit 
Administration.
2. The following portions of Title 6, 
Chapter I, are revoked as obsolete: 
§ 3.23 Functions, powers, authorities,  
and duties of the Director and the  As­
sistant Director of the Regional Agricul­

### T36d65ec92c608acc0504257d

Text hash 4c748d295c7367cfc179ce9be0aee2813e10f1280812143bc0b5b761d7d0400b; absolute characters 11033–11803; files: jobs/source-ladder/output/texts/queue_EO_11343_Emergency_Board_report_to_the_President_on_the_Long_Island_Rail_Road_disputes_EO_1134_fc0b3e43ef.txt

```text
(1) For 12 years beginning in 1954 t.he Carrier became a rail- 
road redevelopment corporation, a new .type of railroad corpora- 
tion as compared with the ordinary corporate status of rail car- 
riers; (2) a 12-year rehabilita.tion program was outlined which 
was estimated to cost $65,613,000; (3) during the 12-year 
redevelopment period the Carrier was given the right to charge 
fares sufficien.t to meet operating expenses plus the cost of the 
improveme~lt program ; (4) during tim first 9 years of the plan, all 
State and local ,taxes, other than on real estate (which previously 
amounted to $500,000 amlua]ly), were eliminated and real estate 
taxes were stabilized a.t less than $1,800,000 annually as compared 
with a previous level in excess of $3,500,000.
```

VOLUME 1 1 'V , 1934 <^>'*  ^a/ited  ^ NUMBER 49
Washington,  Tuesday,  March 12, 1946
The President
EXECUTIVE ORDER 9702
Creating an Emergency Board to Investi ­
gate a D ispute  B etween  T he A lton 
Railroad Company and Other Carriers, 
and C ertain of T heir E mployees
WHEREAS a dispute exists between  
The Alton Railroad Company and other  
carriers, as set forth in the list attached  
hereto and made a part hereof, and cer­
tain of their employees represented by  
the Brotherhood o’ f Locomotive Engi­
neers and the Brotherhood of Railroad  
Trainmen, labor organizations; and  
WHEREAS the dispute has not hereto­
fore been adjusted under the provisions  
of the Railway Labor Act, as amended;  
and
"V ^H E R E A S the dispute, in the judg­
ment of the National Mediation Board,  
threatens substantially to interrupt in­
terstate commerce to a degree such as to  
deprive the country of essential trans­
portation service:
NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of the  
authority vested in me by section 10 of  
the Railway Labor Act, as amended (45  
U.S.C. 160), I hereby create a board of  
three members, to be appointed by me, to 
investigate said dispute. No member of  
the said board shall be pecuniarily or  
otherwise interested in any organization  
of railway employees or any carrier.
The board shall report its findings to  
the President with respect to the said dis­
pute within thirty days from the date of  
this order.
As provide^ by section 10 of the Rail­
way Labor Act, as amended, from this  
date and for thirty days after the board  
has made its report to the President, no  
change, except by agreement, shall be  
made by The Alton Railroad Company  
and the other carriers, or their employees 
in the condition out of which the said  
dispute arose.
Harry S. T ruman 
The W hite  H ouse,
March 8,1946.

Regulations
TITLE 6 —AGRICULTURAL CREDIT
Chapter II— Production and Marketing  
Administration (Commodity Credit) 
[Supp. Announcement 10]
P art 295—D isposal  of S urplus  A gricul­
tural C o m m odities for E xport
SUPPLEMENTAL ANNOUNCEMENT TO TERMS 
AND CONDITIONS OF COTTON SALES FOR 
EXPORT PROGRAM
As stated in the public announcement  
of February 28, 1946, Commodity Credit  
Corporation has amended its Cotton  
Sales for Export Program to change the  
method of computing the price of cotton  
sold from its stocks under the program.  
Accordingly, §  295.4, as amended, of  
the “Terms and Conditions of Cotton  
Sales for Export Program” is deleted and  
the following is substituted:
§  295.4 Purchase price.  The purchase  
price per pound to be paid by an exporter 
for cotton covered by a Purchase Order or 
Purchase Request which is accepted by  
the Corporation shall be the price bid in  
the Purchase Order or Purchase Request, 
less the amount of the applicable export  
differential. The export differential ap­
plicable to any cotton purchased from  
the Corporation hereunder shall be  
the export differential announced by  
Commodity Credit Corporation and in  
effect at the time the Corporation re­
ceives notice of the export sale against  
which such cotton is being purchased.  
If the Purchase Order or Purchase Re­
quest is received by the Corporation more 
than 90 days after the date the Corpora­
tion receives notice of the export sale  
against which such cotton is being pur­
chased, the purchase price shall be in­
creased by 5 points for each month or  
fraction thereof between the end of such 
90-day period and the date the Purchase  
Order or Purchase Request is received by 
■  the corporation. Purchase Orders and  
Purchase Requests shall be submitted  
and construed in accordance with in­
structions issued by th^ New Orleans  
Office of the Cotton Branch, Production  
and Marketing Administration, United 
States Department of Agriculture.
This amendment is effective March 1,  
1946, as to Purchase Orders and Purchase

Presidential Documents
Title 3— THE PRESIDENT
Executive Order 11343
CREATING AN EMERGENCY BOARD TO INVESTIGATE THE DISPUTES
BETWEEN THE LONG ISLAND RAIL ROAD AND CERTAIN OF ITS
EMPLOYEES
WHEREAS, three disputes exist between The Long Island Rail  
Road, a carrier, and certain of its employees represented by the  
Brotherhood of Railroad Trainmen, International Brotherhood of  
Electrical Workers and International Association of Machinists and  
Aerospace Workers, labor organizations;  and
W HEREAS these disputes have not heretofore been adjusted under 
the provisions of the Railway Labor Act, as amended;  and
WHEREAS these disputes, in the judgment of the National Medi­
ation Board, threatens substantially to interrupt interstate commerce  
to such a degree such as to deprive a section of the country of essen­
tial transportation service:
NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of the authority vested in me by  
Section 10 of the Railway Act, as amended (45 U.S.C. 160), I hereby 
create a board of three members, to be appointed by me, to investigate  
these disputes. No member of the board shall be pecuniarily or other­
wise interested in any organization of railroad employees or any carrier.
The board shall report its findings to the President with respect to  
these disputes within 30 days from the date of this order.
As provided by Section 10 of the Railway Labor Act, as amended,  
from this date and for thirty days after the board has made its report  
to the President, no change, except by agreement, shall be made by  
The Long Island Rail Road, or by its employees, in the conditions out  
< ? f which these disputes arose.
T he  W hite  H ouse,
A p ril IV,1967.
[F.R. Doc. 67-4305; Filed, Apr. 17, 1967; 9:29 a.m.]
FEDERAL REGISTER, VOL. 32, NO. 74— TUESDAY, APRIL 18, 1967

```

```text
(1) For 12 years beginning in 1954 t.he Carrier became a rail- 
road redevelopment corporation, a new .type of railroad corpora- 
tion as compared with the ordinary corporate status of rail car- 
riers; (2) a 12-year rehabilita.tion program was outlined which 
was estimated to cost $65,613,000; (3) during the 12-year 
redevelopment period the Carrier was given the right to charge 
fares sufficien.t to meet operating expenses plus the cost of the 
improveme~lt program ; (4) during tim first 9 years of the plan, all 
State and local ,taxes, other than on real estate (which previously 
amounted to $500,000 amlua]ly), were eliminated and real estate 
taxes were stabilized a.t less than $1,800,000 annually as compared 
with a previous level in excess of $3,500,000.
```

After the issuance of Executive Order No.  
9835 by the President, the Department of  
Justice compiled all available data with re­
spect to the type of organization to be dealt  
with under that order. The investigative re­
ports of the Federal Bureau of Investigation  
concerning such organizations were corre­
lated. Memoranda on each such organization  
were prepared by attorneys of the Depart­
ment. The list of organizations contained  
herein has been certified to the Board by the  
Attorney General on the basis of recommen­
dations of attorneys of the Department as  
reviewed by the Solicitor General, the Assist­
ant Attorneys General, and the Assistant  
Solicitor General, and subsequent careful  
study of all by the Attorney General.
In connection with the designation of these  
organizations, the Attorney General has  
pointed out, as the President had done pre­
viously, that it is entirely possible that many  
persons belonging .to such organizations may  
be loyal to the United States; that member­
ship in, affiliation with, or sympathetic asso­
ciation with, any organization designated is  
simply one piece of evidence which may or  
may not be helpful in arriving at a conclu­
sion as to-the action which is to be taken  
in a particular case. “Guilt by association"  
has never been one of the principles of our  
American Jurisprudence. We must be satis­
fied that reasonable grounds exist for con-

THE PRESIDENT 545
Executive Order 11445
CREATING AN EMERGENCY BOARD TO INVESTIGATE DISPUTES BE­
TWEEN THE CARRIERS REPRESENTED BY THE NATIONAL RAILWAY
LABOR CONFERENCE AND CERTAIN OF THEIR EMPLOYEES
WHEREAS disputes exist between the carriers represented by the 
National Railway Labor Conference, designated in List A attached 
hereto and made a part hereof, and certain of their employees repre­
sented by the Brotherhood of Railroad Signalmen, a labor organiza­tion ; and
WHEREAS these disputes have not heretofore been adjusted under 
the provisions of the Railway Labor Act, as amended ; and
WHEREAS these disputes, in the judgment of thè National Media­
tion Board, threaten substantially to interrupt interstate commerce to 
a degree such as to deprive the country of essential transportation 
service:
NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of the authority vested in me by 
Section 10 of the Railway Labor Act, as aihended (45 TJ.S.C. 160), I 
hereby create à board of three members, to be appointed by me, to 
investigate these disputes. No member of the board shall be pecuniarily 
or otherwise interested in any organization of railroad employees or 
any carrier.
The board shall report its findings to the President with respect to 
the disputes within thirty days from the date of this order.
As prpvided by Section 10 of the Railway Labor Act, as amended, 
from this date and for thirty days after the board has made its report 
to the President, no change, except by agreement, shall be made by the 
carriers represented by the National Railway Labor Conference, or by 
their employees, in the conditions out of which the disputes arose.

and text: read in full in the registered Federal Register original (11 FR, issue of 1946-03-12; grade original_representation_unverified, acceptable_as_original true). Order 9702 of March 8, 1946 creates an emergency board of three members under section 10 of the Railway Labor Act for the dispute between the Alton Railroad Company and other carriers and employees represented by the Brotherhood of Locomotive Engineers and the Brotherhood of Railroad Trainmen, directs a report within thirty days of the order, and recites the statutory no-change period of thirty days after the report. Inventory: one sole paragraph. End: EO 12553, signed February 25, 1986, orders, in the official Federal Register text (51 FR 7237, held), that the listed Executive Orders, \"and any amendments thereto, are revoked:\", and its number column lists 9702; the held American Presidency Project transcription (corroboration only, not cited as evidence) pairs 9702 with the subject 'Creating an emergency board to investigate a railroad labor dispute'. Under the owner ruling of 2026-10-21 (report deadlines) the report date and the end of the standstill are spent facts, not ends; the order ends on the express revocation. No held source records when the board reported; if it reported by its deadline of April 7, 1946, the standstill ended by May 7, 1946. Searches: current-law:usc, current-law:ecfr, later-events:ofr_catalog, all complete with no coverage warning.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"The order itself contains no fixed end date and no termination clause. Its report deadline (September 25, 1948, thirty days after signing on August 26, 1948) is a deadline, not an end, and the standstill that runs thirty days after the report is likewise a spent fact under the 2026-10-21 owner ruling; neither is claimed as the end. The actual report date and standstill expiry are not recorded as later events because no reviewed factual-event record for this order's report is in the packet; any report text found in the repository is unreviewed context. The dossier's chain of later events, its three selected machine searches (U.S. Code, eCFR, Federal Register catalog dispositions, each marked complete) and my reading of the held text of Executive Order 12553 show no revoking, superseding or terminating instrument before 1986-02-25.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"Each command (create the board, bar interested members, require the report, freeze conditions) depends on the order and on the dispute it created; none is a separate delegation, continuing regulation or standing rule. The standstill sentence restates section 10 of the Railway Labor Act (45 U.S.C. 160); that statute is an independent source of duty and is untouched by the revocation, but the periods it measures here were set by this order's creation of the board and nothing held shows them still running.\",\"savings_check\":\"Executive Order 12553 was read in full and contains no saving clause. The residual consequences of the order (the board's investigation and report, any settlement of the dispute) are not shown to depend on its continued force. Savings state none_after_bounded_search; section 10 of the Railway Labor Act continues independently as stated in the independent-command check.\",\"scope_check\":\"One clause is inventoried: the whole operative text from the order heading through the standstill sentence (recitals, creation of the board, the bar on interested members, the thirty-day report requirement and the standstill). Signature, date line and any attached list identify the parties and contain no separate command. Executive Order 12553 revokes the order by number as a whole, not in part, so every command in the clause ends on the same date. I read the full held Federal Register text (jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_11343.txt, 32 FR 6085, PDF pages 5-5) and confirmed that its printed number, title and signature date match the census register row. The order has one operative paragraph with no numbered sections and no stated end date, so the dossier's single whole-order clause is the complete inventory (any carrier list the order attaches is an incorporated list, not a separate command). EO 12553 (signed 1986-02-25; held text jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt) revokes the order by number in its table. The first end is that express revocation. The report deadline, the report itself and the end of the standstill are not ends under the owner ruling of 2026-10-21 on report deadlines, and no held reviewed source establishes the dates on which the board reported or the standstill ran out. Bounded search read for this order: the dossier's three machine searches (U.S. Code release point 119-121 through 2026-10-09; eCFR and GovInfo CFR bulk through 2026-10-14; Federal Register catalog disposition notes and operative sentences of 17,521 saved order texts), after reading their coverage notes, which carry no coverage warning; the Cruden by-number scan of held order texts in the packet's LOOKUPS.md; and a full read of EO 12553 (held FR text, 33,227 characters).\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Own terms: the order states a report deadline (\\\"within 30 days from the date of this order\\\") and a standstill running (\\\"for thirty days after the board has made its report\\\"). A report deadline is a deadline, not an end, and the standstill is a spent fact whose date no held source fixes; neither is recorded as an end. The order states no date on which it ends. Held later instruments: the by-number scan lists no held later order that cites this order other than EO 12553, which names it in its revocation table; the Federal Register catalog note records the EO 12553 revocation and the U.S. Code and eCFR searches return no hit. No unnamed statute or order was found that would be an earlier candidate end beyond section 10 of the Railway Labor Act, the statute the order itself invokes.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"Independent commands: the order (1) creates the board and bars members with a financial or organizational interest, (2) directs a report within thirty days and (3) recites the section 10 standstill. Command (3) rests on the statute, which binds the parties for its own thirty-day period regardless of the order, so it is not an independent surviving command of the order. The order makes no appointment by name, creates no fund or property right and delegates no continuing authority, so nothing in it outlives its revocation.\",\"savings_check\":\"Savings: no savings language in EO 12553 or in this order; the completed facts are spent and nothing continues independently.\",\"scope_check\":\"Whole-order scope: EO 12553 revokes the order in full. Its table lists the order by number with no 'in part' or section limitation, so the single clause ends on the revocation date, and the held sources record no partial amendment of this order.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[{"file_sha256":"55551fd7e9cfcbf6f6c8af1c0795595628a981b72c538f5c09769427f86db049","path":"research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g08/packet/complete-v1/receipts/research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g08/packet/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:14","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"rejected","version_binding":"exact"},{"file_sha256":"55551fd7e9cfcbf6f6c8af1c0795595628a981b72c538f5c09769427f86db049","path":"research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g08/packet/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:14","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"rejected","version_binding":"exact"}],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:11343#whole"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":["first_end"],"scope_id":"EO:11343#whole"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt","sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761","state":"match"},"sha256":"945348afbba863ae7dc1fbe7df8759682fe1733962734a9255a1062c0561048c","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt","text_sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"e52c343b97e687b9779972a619e323d1e7d0a78d4f25187330618cdc8cedd814","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_11343.txt","sha256":"e52c343b97e687b9779972a619e323d1e7d0a78d4f25187330618cdc8cedd814","state":"match"},"sha256":"e73f3bb6b7fefb2ce98accf85b5e4dd461650cf209f3745651ba85290a079f76","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_11343.txt","text_sha256":"e52c343b97e687b9779972a619e323d1e7d0a78d4f25187330618cdc8cedd814"}]},{"claim_sha256":"f84287ae5b45b876d7212b6518738cc10b66ce9636b2dc920712edc903701304","current_canonical":true,"locations":[{"file_sha256":"19f2917a7cb9259b74d68cad12eac61059c1a637d325227c87057f95377d8da9","path":"research/readiness/claims/claude-2026-11-04-tribonian-f02.jsonl","pointer":"line:15"}],"reported_material_gaps":[],"research_rationale":"Identity: the held Federal Register text (32 FR 6085, the issue of April 18, 1967) is the order itself: it is headed Executive Order 11343, creates an emergency board under section 10 of the Railway Labor Act for the dispute described in the clause, and is dated April 12, 1967. I read the whole order; the representation is the machine-extracted text layer of the official PDF (OCR for this period), and I did not inspect the page images. End: express revocation by Executive Order 12553 on 1986-02-25, under the owner ruling of 2026-10-21 on report deadlines (option C): a report deadline and a spent standstill are not ends. Searches: current-law:usc, current-law:ecfr and later-events:ofr_catalog, each marked complete in the dossier; the U.S. Code and eCFR searches found no hit naming this order. The held-text number search for this order found no held later order that cites it other than Executive Order 12553.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"The order contains no fixed end date and no termination clause. Its commands are a bar on interested board members, a requirement that the board report within thirty days from the date of the order (due by May 12, 1967), and a standstill running from the date of the order until thirty days after the report. The report deadline and the standstill are spent facts, not ends, under the owner ruling of 2026-10-21 on report deadlines, and neither is claimed as the end. A held lead copy of the report of Emergency Board No. 170 (a text of the front matter of the National Mediation Board publication, graded held_text_lead, a grade the claim format does not accept as a cited source) is dated May 12, 1967. Thirty days after May 12, 1967 is June 11, 1967. Those dates come from a lead and are disclosed, not claimed as events or as the end: the clause is described only as functionally spent on May 12, 1967, the report date, with the standstill functionally spent on June 11, 1967. My reading of the held texts of Executive Order 12553 and the number search of the held texts show no earlier revoking, superseding or terminating instrument for this order.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"Each command (create the board, bar interested members, require the report, freeze conditions) depends on this order and on the dispute it created; none is a separate delegation, continuing regulation or standing rule. The standstill sentence applies section 10 of the Railway Labor Act (45 U.S.C. 160); the statute is a separate source of law that an Executive order does not revoke, but the periods measured here were set by this order's creation of the board, and no held claim-grade source shows them still running when the order was revoked.\",\"savings_check\":\"Executive Order 12553 was read in full: it consists of one revoking sentence, a table of the revoked orders by number and subject, an editorial note and the signature block, and it contains no saving or transition provision. No residual effect of this order is shown to depend on its continued force. Section 10 of the Railway Labor Act continues independently as stated in the independent-command check.\",\"scope_check\":\"One clause is inventoried: the operative text from the order heading through the standstill sentence (the recitals, the creation of the board of three members, the bar on interested members, the report requirement and the standstill). The signature, the date line and any attached lists follow it; the attached lists, where present, only identify carriers and labor organizations and contain no command. Executive Order 12553 revokes the order by number as a whole, not in part, so every command in the clause ends on the same date. The ending source cited is the revoking sentence and table of Executive Order 12553.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:11343#whole"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:11343#whole"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt","sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761","state":"match"},"sha256":"945348afbba863ae7dc1fbe7df8759682fe1733962734a9255a1062c0561048c","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt","text_sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"e52c343b97e687b9779972a619e323d1e7d0a78d4f25187330618cdc8cedd814","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_11343.txt","sha256":"e52c343b97e687b9779972a619e323d1e7d0a78d4f25187330618cdc8cedd814","state":"match"},"sha256":"e73f3bb6b7fefb2ce98accf85b5e4dd461650cf209f3745651ba85290a079f76","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_11343.txt","text_sha256":"e52c343b97e687b9779972a619e323d1e7d0a78d4f25187330618cdc8cedd814"}]}]}

11445 (1969-01-13, 34 FR 545) creates an emergency board under section 10 of the Railway Labor Act to investigate disputes between the carriers represented by the National Railway Labor Conference and certain of their employees represented by the Brotherhood of Railroad Signalmen, directs the board to report within thirty days, and recites the statutory standstill that section 10 imposes on the parties from the order's date until thirty days after the report. I read the full held Federal Register text (jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_11445.txt, 34 FR 545, PDF pages 9-10) and confirmed that its printed number, title and signature date match the census register row. The order has one operative paragraph with no numbered sections and no stated end date, so the dossier's single whole-order clause is the complete inventory (any carrier list the order attaches is an incorporated list, not a separate command). EO 12553 (signed 1986-02-25; held text jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt) revokes the order by number in its table. The first end is that express revocation. The report deadline, the report itself and the end of the standstill are not ends under the owner ruling of 2026-10-21 on report deadlines, and no held reviewed source establishes the dates on which the board reported or the standstill ran out. Bounded search read for this order: the dossier's three machine searches (U.S. Code release point 119-121 through 2026-10-09; eCFR and GovInfo CFR bulk through 2026-10-14; Federal Register catalog disposition notes and operative sentences of 17,521 saved order texts), after reading their coverage notes, which carry no coverage warning; the Cruden by-number scan of held order texts in the packet's LOOKUPS.md; and a full read of EO 12553 (held FR text, 33,227 characters).\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Own terms: the order states a report deadline (\\\"within thirty days from the date of this order\\\") and a standstill running (\\\"for thirty days after the board has made its report\\\"). A report deadline is a deadline, not an end, and the standstill is a spent fact whose date no held source fixes; neither is recorded as an end. The order states no date on which it ends. Held later instruments: the by-number scan lists no held later order that cites this order other than EO 12553, which names it in its revocation table; the Federal Register catalog note records the EO 12553 revocation and the U.S. Code and eCFR searches return no hit. No unnamed statute or order was found that would be an earlier candidate end beyond section 10 of the Railway Labor Act, the statute the order itself invokes.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"Independent commands: the order (1) creates the board and bars members with a financial or organizational interest, (2) directs a report within thirty days and (3) recites the section 10 standstill. Command (3) rests on the statute, which binds the parties for its own thirty-day period regardless of the order, so it is not an independent surviving command of the order. The order makes no appointment by name, creates no fund or property right and delegates no continuing authority, so nothing in it outlives its revocation.\",\"savings_check\":\"Savings: no savings language in EO 12553 or in this order; the completed facts are spent and nothing continues independently.\",\"scope_check\":\"Whole-order scope: EO 12553 revokes the order in full. Its table lists the order by number with no 'in part' or section limitation, so the single clause ends on the revocation date, and the held sources record no partial amendment of this order.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[{"file_sha256":"55551fd7e9cfcbf6f6c8af1c0795595628a981b72c538f5c09769427f86db049","path":"research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g08/packet/complete-v1/receipts/research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g08/packet/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:21","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"upheld","version_binding":"exact"},{"file_sha256":"55551fd7e9cfcbf6f6c8af1c0795595628a981b72c538f5c09769427f86db049","path":"research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g08/packet/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:21","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"upheld","version_binding":"exact"}],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:11445#whole"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:11445#whole"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt","sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761","state":"match"},"sha256":"945348afbba863ae7dc1fbe7df8759682fe1733962734a9255a1062c0561048c","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt","text_sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"8e78dd9c1bdf04de3795ab4f4ad544222cdfb2e5b5d6274c83c8588aa7a386ec","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_11445.txt","sha256":"8e78dd9c1bdf04de3795ab4f4ad544222cdfb2e5b5d6274c83c8588aa7a386ec","state":"match"},"sha256":"b59fa935f2126e0289c24caec5cf18378a0292089f264caf273c5650c44794b6","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_11445.txt","text_sha256":"8e78dd9c1bdf04de3795ab4f4ad544222cdfb2e5b5d6274c83c8588aa7a386ec"}]}]}

## Valla review leads and component attestations

Review lead only. Every case needs separate applicability, earlier-ending, scope, independent-command, savings and independent whole-case review.
Preparation attestations never approve legal conclusions or replace whole-case review.
